\title{Form Neutrino Masses: From Neutral Depth to a Planck-Ratio Normal Hierarchy}
\author{Johannes Michael Wielsch\\
\small Literate Agda paper in the \emph{Void and Form} programme}
\date{May 2026}

\begin{document}
\maketitle

\begin{abstract}
This paper gives a machine-checked rational skeleton for a normal-hierarchy
neutrino mass readout inside the \emph{Void and Form} invariant ledger.  A
finite $K_4$ candidate space is reduced to the neutral-depth survivor
$d+\chi=5$; an electron-anchored reading is retained as a comparison layer;
and a Planck-anchored mass chain is classified through route and exponent
filters, a running correction, a sign-ordered correction tower,
charged-sector correction data, normal-hierarchy observables, and the neutral
residual carrier.  The residual carrier is the coupling complement $295/306$,
so the final neutral separator is
$m_3/m_2 = 6\cdot 295/306 = 1770/306$.  With the displayed Planck input this
gives
$m_1=0$, $m_2\approx 0.008589080150\,\mathrm{eV}$,
$m_3\approx 0.049681934200\,\mathrm{eV}$, and
$\sum_i m_i\approx 0.058271014350\,\mathrm{eV}$.  The Agda content is the
finite integer and rational chain.  The assertion that this chain is the
neutrino mass spectrum of nature remains an empirical identification.
\end{abstract}

\section*{Introduction}

Oscillation experiments measure neutrino mass splittings, but the absolute
mass scale remains outside the Standard Model.  A serious prediction of that
scale must do more than write down small numbers.  It must specify the finite
candidate space, show which candidates are eliminated, state every external
physical input, and separate the checked arithmetic from the empirical claim
that the resulting chain describes the world.

The construction below follows that discipline.  The first formal output is
the neutral-depth square $5^2=25$.  The electron-anchored reading is retained
as a low-energy comparison layer.  The Planck-anchored layer then classifies
the route to the electron scale, the suppression exponents, the running
factor, the correction tower action, the charged-sector correction data, and
the normal-hierarchy observables.  None of those choices is selected by
decimal agreement with measured neutrino masses; the decimals are read only
after the rational chain has been fixed.

The charged and neutral residual layers are treated differently.  The charged
mass ratios still carry structured ppm-level residuals whose sign pattern
belongs to the physical comparison with hadronic and leptonic sectors.  The
neutral splitting residual is classified at the carrier level.  Matching
weights are blocked by $\mathrm{Aut}(K_4)$-uniformity, so the residual acts
one layer above the matchings.  The surviving carrier is the coupling
complement $q-p$ over $q$, with $q=306$, $p=11$, and $q-p=295$.

\section*{Scientific claim and validation}

The formal claim of this paper is narrow: within the displayed finite
candidate spaces, the rational chain from neutral depth to final Planck
ratios is mechanically checked in Agda under \texttt{--safe} and
\texttt{--without-K}.  The physical claim is stronger and remains exposed:
the exact rational readout is proposed as the normal neutrino mass hierarchy
of nature.

For the paper to count as a scientific contribution rather than a formal
curiosity, six obligations must remain visible.

\begin{enumerate}
\item The Agda file must type-check from a clean checkout, without postulates
  or unsafe flags.
\item The candidate spaces must be explicit enough that the survivor is not a
  fitted choice hidden behind notation.
\item The Planck input, unit conventions, and low-energy comparison constants
  must be named rather than absorbed into the derivation.
\item The exact rational outputs must be stated before the electronvolt
  decimals are compared with data.
\item The comparison with oscillation data, cosmological mass bounds, beta
  decay, and neutrinoless-double-beta searches must be reproducible from the
  displayed numbers.
\item The failure conditions must be explicit: an incompatible hierarchy,
  nonzero lightest mass beyond the stated boundary, a conflicting mass sum, or
  squared splittings outside the proposed readout would refute the physical
  identification, not the formal arithmetic.
\end{enumerate}

\section*{Structure of the paper}

The large programme is a dependency chain.  It starts at the boundary
argument for distinction, crosses the formal threshold only after the
binary datum has been supplied, and ends only when a dimensionful mass
number can be compared with experiment.  Every link must preserve the
status of the previous link.

The intended route is:
\[
\begin{array}{c}
\mathrm{distinction}
\to \mathrm{binary\ normal\ form}
\to K_4\,\mathrm{closure}
\to \mathrm{invariant\ ledger} \\
\to \mathrm{number\ tower}
\to \mathrm{spectral/operator\ layer}
\to \mathrm{sector\ assignment} \\
\to \mathrm{mass\ ratios}
\to \mathrm{correction/running}
\to \mathrm{absolute\ scale}
\to \mathrm{particle\ masses}.
\end{array}
\]

The paper follows that route in the following order.

\begin{enumerate}
\item Use the distinction-to-$K_4$ kernel supplied by \emph{Void} without
  reopening its proof.
\item Import only the small invariant ledger needed for the neutral
  problem: $V,E,d,\chi,\kappa$, the Fermat slots, loop numerator, and
  loop volumes.
\item Classify neutral-depth candidates and prove the finite survivor
  statement for depth $5$ and denominator $25$.
\item Record the electron-anchored neutrino prediction as a physical
  comparison candidate, because the charged-mass ledger is expressed in
  ratios to $m_e$.
\item Classify the Planck-to-low-energy bridge.  The bridge must explain
  the large suppression from the Planck unit to the electron and neutral
  scale without inserting a tunable parameter.
\item State the resulting Planck-ratio particle masses and the neutrino
  observables that follow from them.
\item Compare the resulting masses with experiment, including charged
  leptons, quarks where the scheme applies, neutrino splittings, total
  neutrino mass, beta-decay bounds, and neutrinoless-double-beta limits.
  This comparison is not an Agda theorem; it is the empirical boundary.
\end{enumerate}

  The canonical programme instance fills the ledger.  Every proof field is
  `refl`, so the object is inspectable as a closed table: no later prose can
  quietly upgrade a physical-test field into a checked theorem.

\section{The \texorpdfstring{$K_4$}{K4} ledger used by the schema}

The record below is deliberately small.  It names only the invariant
slots needed by the neutral-depth and Planck-suppression filters:
vertices, edges, degree, Euler boundary, the boundary unit, the
Fermat-prime slot, the discrete $\kappa$ scale, the closure integer, the
universal loop numerator, and the two loop volumes already used
elsewhere in the project.  These are formal ledger entries, not physical
names.

\section{Finite neutral-depth candidates}

The schema does not start with a mass value.  It starts with four local
depth candidates already visible in the ledger:

\begin{itemize}
\item the generation depth, $d = 3$;
\item the closure-carrier depth, $V = 4$;
\item the neutral depth, $d + \chi = 5$;
\item the edge-sector depth, $E = 6$.
\end{itemize}

The neutral slot is asked to lie beyond the generation count, not to be
identical with the closure carrier itself, and not to be the full edge
sector.  Those three filters are the formal admission data of this
schema.  They are intentionally weaker than a physical claim:
they only say which candidate survives the displayed classification.

\section{The neutral-depth filter}

The elimination is finite.  The generation slot fails because it has
not moved beyond the charged generation count.  The closure slot fails
because it is the already-used four-case carrier.  The edge slot fails
because it belongs to the full edge sector.  Only the neutral depth
survives all three filters.

\section{Complete schema interface}

The complete schema packages the survivor, the fact that it survives,
its value, and its uniqueness against every other candidate in the
finite window.  The depth square is the formal output of this section;
the later Planck and residual layers use it as input rather than
reopening the neutral-depth classification.

\section{Planck-to-low-energy bridge classification}

The bridge problem can now be stated without drama.  The Planck anchor
is available as the natural ultraviolet unit, and the neutral denominator
is available from the finite schema.  The first predicate records why the
uncorrected routes do not suffice: direct Planck division stays in the
ultraviolet sector; the Higgs route names an intermediate scale without
deriving the running; the bare electron route carries only the structured
suppression denominator; and the electron-only route uses a low-energy
physical anchor.

The corrected predicate then adds the missing structure.  It requires
the Planck-suppression survivor, the running survivor, the complete
correction-tower interface, the canonical tower action, and the absence
of an empirical patch.  In this finite candidate space, only the
sign-ordered tower reading survives.  This is the formal bridge used in
the mass-chain section below.

The route fixes only the scale path.  The suppression still needs exponents.
The first exponent is the $\kappa$ slot: among the boundary, Euler,
degree, and carrier candidates, only the Euler exponent survives.

therefore $4+3=7$.

The three exponent filters are then packaged as one suppression schema.  This
record is the formal reason the denominator below is a product of the
neutral slot, $8^2$, $17^4$, and $137^7$.

The uncorrected suppression is not yet the final bridge.  The running filter
requires the loop remainder, the RG scale, and the generation depth, while
blocking volume substitution.  This leaves the factor $833/822$.

The correction tower contributes four signed slots.  The first two raise the
bridge, the last two lower it; the point is not a fitted decimal but a fixed
signed tower action.

The tower interface still leaves possible actions.  The finite action filter
requires both signs, preserves their order, and refuses sector naming as a
choice.  Only the sign-ordered full action survives.

The surviving action has a single rational factor.  The following block shows
the positive face, the negative face, and the product which enters the final
Planck-ratio masses.

Before the corrected bridge is admitted, the paper records that no bare
Planck bridge reaches the low-energy scale.  This is the guardrail that keeps
the correction tower from looking optional.

The structured candidate records the denominator and every locked slot in the
Planck-to-electron suppression.  It is not yet the final low-energy bridge;
it is the checked structured candidate before the final reading filter.

The final low-energy bridge candidate must combine all preceding pieces:
Planck suppression, running, complete tower interface, canonical tower action,
and no empirical patch.  This is the bridge used by the mass prediction.

The corrected Planck bridge also audits the correction chain.  The running
factor must use the loop remainder and the RG denominator without replacing
the bridge by a volume correction.  The tower must use the sign-ordered action
without letting sector names choose the result.  The final low-energy bridge
must close without an empirical patch.  This is where the QFT/QCD reading is
kept in its proper place: it can motivate the physical language of loop
correction, but the neutral mass chain inherits only the formal loop grammar.

\section{Exact electron-anchored prediction candidate}

The Planck scale is the natural scale for the ultraviolet cutoff: in the
discrete reading, one lattice edge may be read as one Planck length and
the corresponding cutoff energy as one Planck mass.  This fixes the unit
in which a low-energy particle mass is measured; it does not select such
a mass by itself.  If the denominator below were applied directly to the
Planck mass, the result would be a Planck-sector
excitation, not a neutrino-scale mass: $M_P/(4\cdot137^3) \approx
1.19\cdot10^{21}\,\mathrm{eV}$.  To obtain a $0.05\,\mathrm{eV}$
neutrino directly from $M_P$, the ledger would need an additional
suppression of about $2.46\cdot10^{29}$.  The Planck-to-low-energy
bridge supplying that suppression is classified in the Planck-anchored
layer; the electron-anchored layer is retained as a comparison reading.

The exact prediction needs one physical scale.  The choice made here is
the electron mass, because the mass ledger developed here expresses
charged-particle masses as ratios to $m_e$.  The neutral prediction is
therefore not an electron-volt theorem.  It is an exact dimensionless
claim:
\[
  m_1 = 0, \qquad
  \frac{m_2}{m_e} = \frac{1}{6\cdot 4\cdot 137^3}, \qquad
  \frac{m_3}{m_e} = \frac{1}{4\cdot 137^3}.
\]
Here $137$ is the closure integer, the exponent $3$ is the generation
depth, $4$ is the $K_4$ carrier, and $6$ is the edge-sector separation
between the two nonzero neutral masses.  The neutral-depth interface
above remains present: it locks the admissible neutral slot at depth $5$
with square $25$.  The scale formula below is the additional physical
reading that turns the slot into an absolute mass proposal.

Using $m_e = 510998.950\,\mathrm{eV}$ only as the external scale, this
candidate gives
\[
  m_1 = 0, \qquad
  m_2 = 0.008280318928\,\mathrm{eV}, \qquad
  m_3 = 0.049681913568\,\mathrm{eV},
\]
and
\[
  \sum_i m_i = 0.057962232497\,\mathrm{eV}.
\]
Those decimals are not hard-coded below; the checked content is the
integer denominator pattern and the boundary level of the scale anchor.

The candidate space below prevents that separator from being smuggled in as
the desired number: boundary, Euler, degree, vertices, and edges are all
tested against the same four filters.

The four filters are load-bearing.  The essentiality witnesses show what
would go wrong if any one condition were dropped: boundary, vertices, degree,
or Euler would re-enter as a rival.

the external electron scale.  The zero, middle, heaviest, and summed entries
remain exact ratios.

\section{Planck-anchored mass-chain}

The Planck-anchored chain begins with the survivor of a finite route
classification and three finite slot classifications.  The route is
forced to the Planck-to-electron bridge; the $\kappa$ exponent is forced
to the Euler slot; the Fermat exponent is forced to the carrier slot; and
the closure exponent is forced to the carrier-plus-generation slot.
Together with the already proved neutral survivor this gives the
canonical denominator
\[
  5\cdot 8^2\cdot 17^4\cdot 137^7
\]
without using the decimal comparison as a filter.

Read by itself, this bare survivor already gives exact rational
multiples of $M_P$.  With $M_P \approx
1.22089\cdot 10^{28}\,\mathrm{eV}$ it yields, before the running and
tower corrections, approximately
\[
  m_e = 504297.6015\,\mathrm{eV},\quad
  m_p = 925.8904\,\mathrm{MeV},\quad
  m_\mu = 104.3896\,\mathrm{MeV},\quad
  m_\tau = 1774.6233\,\mathrm{MeV},
\]
and
\[
  m_3 = 0.0490303744\,\mathrm{eV},\quad
  m_2 = 0.0081717291\,\mathrm{eV},\quad
  \sum_i m_i = 0.0572021035\,\mathrm{eV}.
\]
These numbers are consequences of the classified survivor.  They are not
the reason it was selected.  They are also not the final reading of the
chain: the bare bridge lacks the running and tower action that the next
filters require.

The running gate is also classified rather than fitted.  The survivor is
the degree-weighted RG loop correction
\[
  \frac{d\cdot 274 + 11}{d\cdot 274} = \frac{833}{822}.
\]
Applied to the bare Planck chain, it gives the running-corrected
consequences
\[
  m_e = 511046.1096\,\mathrm{eV},\quad
  m_p = 938.2807\,\mathrm{MeV},\quad
  m_\mu = 105.7865\,\mathrm{MeV},\quad
  m_\tau = 1798.3713\,\mathrm{MeV},
\]
and
\[
  m_3 = 0.0496864987\,\mathrm{eV},\quad
  m_2 = 0.0082810831\,\mathrm{eV},\quad
  \sum_i m_i = 0.0579675818\,\mathrm{eV}.
\]
These corrected numbers are again consequences, not filters.  The
running-only stage is intermediate: the full correction tower follows.

The correction tower has a finite interface: two positive terms
\[
  \frac{2}{137^2},\quad \frac{1}{8\cdot 3\cdot 137^2}
\]
and two negative terms
\[
  -\frac{3^2}{8\cdot 137^2},\quad
  -\frac{(11+2)^2}{8^2\cdot 137^2}.
\]
The sign-forced action is classified next: the two positive terms act
first, then the two negative terms.  This gives the exact tower factor
\[
  \frac{1524775277821401191187}{1524915363442855784448}.
\]
Composed with the running-corrected Planck chain, this yields
\[
  m_e = 510999.1626\,\mathrm{eV},\quad
  m_p = 938.1945\,\mathrm{MeV},\quad
  m_\mu = 105.7768\,\mathrm{MeV},\quad
  m_\tau = 1798.2061\,\mathrm{MeV},
\]
and
\[
  m_3 = 0.0496819342\,\mathrm{eV},\quad
  m_2 = 0.0082803224\,\mathrm{eV},\quad
  \sum_i m_i = 0.0579622566\,\mathrm{eV}.
\]
The tower-level normal-hierarchy observables are therefore fixed as exact
squared Planck ratios before the neutral residual carrier is applied.  In
eV units, using the same Planck input, they read approximately
\[
  \Delta m^2_{21} = 6.8564\cdot 10^{-5}\,\mathrm{eV}^2,
  \qquad
  \Delta m^2_{31} = 2.4683\cdot 10^{-3}\,\mathrm{eV}^2.
\]
At this point the formal bridge closes at the level of the displayed
filters.  Among the finite low-energy readings, only the sign-ordered
tower action uses the Planck-suppression survivor, the running survivor,
the complete tower interface, and no empirical patch.  The residual
carrier classification below supplies the final neutral separator; the
empirical boundary is the comparison of the resulting Planck ratios with
measured masses and neutrino data.

The charged tiers then receive their sector corrections.  The proton
uses $1836 + 11/72 = 132203/72$, the muon uses
$207 - 16/69 = 14267/69$, and the tau/muon tier uses
$17 - 28/153 = 2573/153$.  Applied after the formal Planck bridge, these
give the charged-mass reading
\[
  m_p = 938.2725\,\mathrm{MeV},\quad
  m_\mu = 105.6583\,\mathrm{MeV},\quad
  m_\tau = 1776.8555\,\mathrm{MeV}.
\]
Again, the displayed decimals are consequences of the rational chain;
the filters select the fractions before any measured mass is consulted.

The sector corrections are rational refinements of the charged ratios.  The
proton receives $+11/72$, the muon receives $-16/69$, and the tau/muon tier
receives $-28/153$.

to electron, proton, muon, tau, and the two nonzero neutral masses.

The tower action now multiplies the running-corrected electron bridge.  The
same corrected bridge is then used for the charged particles and for the two
neutral denominators.

The separator remains invariant under the running and tower layers because
those corrections act uniformly on the shared Planck bridge.  This matters:
the neutral splitting ratio is not re-fitted after the tower is applied.

oscillation data enter this block.

The candidate record collects the bridge, corrections, charged ratios and
neutral observables into one closed object.  Its fields are long because it
acts as the ledger sheet for the full Planck-anchored chain.

\begin{code}%
\>[0]\AgdaComment{--\ \$\ PlanckAnchoredParticleMassCandidate.}\<%

\AgdaKeyword{where}\<%

\>[2]\AgdaKeyword{field}\<%
\\
\>[2][@{}l@{\AgdaIndent{0}}]%
\>[4]\AgdaField{bridge-candidate}%
\>[33]\AgdaSymbol{:}\AgdaSpace{}%
\AgdaRecord{StructuredPlanckElectronCandidate}\<%

claims reduce by `refl`, and the uncorrected bridge remains eliminated.

\section{Residual layer: forced kind and carrier boundary}

The first-order corrections classified above leave a structured residual
when the rational ratios are read against precision data.  Two
observations are taken as input here, not as choices.  In the charged
sector the ppm-level residual carries a sign asymmetry: the hadronic
ratio $p/e$ misses on the negative side, while both leptonic ratios
$\mu/e$ and $\tau/e$ miss on the positive side.  In the neutral sector
both squared-mass differences miss on the positive side, but their
relative shifts are not equal: a single multiplicative factor on
$m_\nu$ would preserve $\Delta m^2_{31}/\Delta m^2_{21}$, and that ratio
is not preserved.

The residual layer is therefore classified at the level of \emph{kind}.
Three candidate kinds are admitted formally and one is forced by the
data.  In the charged sector, a global Planck-scale shift and a uniform
running factor would impose a single sign on all charged residuals; both
are eliminated by the observed Proton/Lepton sign split.  Only a
\emph{sector-asymmetric} residual kind survives.  In the neutral sector,
a single multiplicative shift on the neutrino mass scale is eliminated
by the non-unit splitting ratio; only a \emph{splitting-asymmetric}
residual kind survives.

The kind classification is the first formal output of this layer.  In
the charged sector it records a physical comparison boundary: the sign
split is visible, but no exact charged residual carrier is selected by
decimal matching.  In the neutral sector the carrier is classified below
by the coupling complement.  Thus the charged residual is
kept as \texttt{physical-test}, while the neutral residual is carried to
a formal ratio.

The neutrino residual is different.  It is not merely a sign asymmetry in a
physical comparison table; the splitting-asymmetric carrier is later closed
by the coupling complement.

\section{Required-input classification for the carrier}

The kind classification fixes \emph{what} the residual layer must do.
It does not fix \emph{which} ledger slot carries it.  Rather than
guessing a slot by decimal matching, we classify the minimal structural
input that any carrier candidate must provide before it can close.  The
candidate space is intentionally small and exhaustive at the level of
admissible inputs.

The data fix two structural axes for the charged sector: a hadronic
versus leptonic sign split, and a $\mu$ versus $\tau$ magnitude split.
For the neutral sector the data fix one axis: a $\Delta m^2_{21}$
versus $\Delta m^2_{31}$ magnitude split.  An admissible input must
provide the corresponding resolution.  The empirical patch input is
globally excluded by the bridge classification above.

Two clean structural facts follow.  The charged carrier requires both
\texttt{sign-resolution} and \texttt{generation-resolution}; a
higher-order-tower input alone does not produce a sign split, and a
sign-resolution input alone does not separate $\mu$ from $\tau$.  The
neutral carrier requires \texttt{generation-resolution}; a uniform
neutral shift cannot reproduce the non-unit splitting ratio.  The
\texttt{higher-order-tower} input remains admissible only as a
combination partner, never as a sole carrier.  The
\texttt{empirical-patch} input is forbidden on principle.

The basic $K_4$ ledger used up to this point does not carry an internal
hadronic/leptonic $\mathbf{Z}_2$ distinction.  The carrier closure therefore
requires a genuine ledger extension, not a recycling of occupied slots.  The
next section supplies that extension from the
intrinsic bipartitions and perfect matchings of $K_4$.

The neutral admissible input is therefore not a singleton: both the
pure generation-resolution input and the combined input satisfy the
neutral filter.  The next predicate selects the \emph{minimal}
admissible input by adding the requirement that no extra resolution is
imported.  In this finite candidate space, only the pure
generation-resolution input survives.

The next predicate records that the basic ledger does not carry the
hadronic/leptonic distinction internally.  This is a single boolean slot
rather than a derivation, because the ledger exposed so far has no such
distinction; the field keeps that limitation explicit.

The combination is the formal output of this section.  The charged
carrier requires the combined input; the neutral carrier requires the
generation-resolution input as the minimal admissible input; and the
ledger as first displayed does not provide the sign split internally.
The following extension resolves that structural requirement without
using a fit.

\section{Internal ledger extension: bipartition and matching}

The previous section recorded that the carrier closure requires a
ledger extension.  The next finite question is: which extension is
admissible without importing structure foreign to $K_4$?  Two intrinsic
$K_4$ refinements are available before any external object is
considered.  First, the four vertices admit a bipartition into two
pairs; the six edges then split into four cross edges and two internal
edges.  This is a $\mathbf{Z}_2$ refinement of the vertex set, and it
is intrinsic to $K_4$.  Second, $K_4$ admits three perfect matchings,
each consisting of two disjoint edges; this is an intrinsic three-fold
refinement of the edge set.  Together they provide a sign resolution
and a generation resolution drawn entirely from $K_4$ itself.

The candidate space below is exhaustive at the level of where the
extension is taken from.  An empirical flag is forbidden by the
discipline established above.  A foreign group adds a structure that is
not visible in $K_4$ and would be a free choice.  The pure bipartition
provides a sign resolution but no generation refinement.  The pure
matching set provides a generation refinement but no sign resolution.
Only the joint intrinsic extension provides both, and it does so
without importing anything outside $K_4$.

The intrinsic extension carries two integer counts that are forced by
$K_4$ alone: the number of vertex bipartitions and the number of
perfect matchings.  A bipartition of four vertices into two unordered
pairs is determined by choosing two vertices for one side and dividing
out the swap, giving three.  Each perfect matching of $K_4$ is in
bijection with such a bipartition by choosing the two cross edges that
share no vertex, giving three again.  Both counts equal three, and
this coincidence is the formal source of the three-fold refinement
used by the carrier.

The closure record below packages the intrinsic extension with the
carrier-closure requirement.  After this point the residual carrier
problem reduces to the physical assignment of which side of the
bipartition is the hadronic side and which matching is the first
generation.  That assignment is a physical reading and is therefore
recorded as \texttt{physical-test}, not as a formal theorem.

\section{Cell structure forced by the intrinsic extension}

The intrinsic extension produces a finite cell table.  Each cell is a
pair of a bipartition side and a matching index.  Two sides times three
matchings gives six cells, and the six edges of $K_4$ provide the
counting witness: each edge belongs to exactly one matching and joins
exactly one ordered pair of bipartition sides.  The cell count and the
edge count therefore coincide as a structural identity, not as a
numerical fit.

The cell table has a forced internal structure: each side carries
exactly three cells, indexed by the three matchings.  In the leptonic
sector this matches the three observed charged leptons.  In the
hadronic sector the structure forces three generation slots, of which
the proton observable occupies one.  The remaining two hadronic slots
are predictions of the cell table, not data.  They are recorded here as
formal slots whose physical content is a separate identification.

The next predicate names the observed cells.  By the discipline
established above, the side and matching assignment is a physical
reading.  Naming it here is therefore a record of \emph{which physical
content is consistent with the cell structure}, not a derivation.  The
proton occupies one hadronic slot; the electron, muon and tau occupy
the three leptonic slots.  The two unfilled hadronic slots are
predictions of the cell structure that the first-order ledger leaves to
physical identification against measured masses.

\section{Automorphism invariance forbids matching-differential weights}

The intrinsic carrier closure forces three perfect matchings on $K_4$
and assigns the three observed leptonic generations to those three
matchings.  In principle one could try to absorb the residual ratio
gap between $m_3/m_2 = 6$ and the experimental value $\sqrt{33.55}
\approx 5.79$ by giving each matching its own multiplicative weight.
The block below classifies that move and rules it out at the formal
level.  $\mathrm{Aut}(K_4) = S_4$ acts transitively on the three
perfect matchings; any K\textsubscript{4}-intrinsic weight assignment
that depends on the matching index breaks $\mathrm{Aut}(K_4)$
invariance.  Under the joint conditions
\emph{$K_4$-intrinsic, $\mathrm{Aut}(K_4)$-invariant, no external
generator}, the only admissible weight pattern is uniform across all
three matchings.  Hence no purely intrinsic mechanism can produce a
matching-differential factor on the neutral pair.

\begin{code}%
\>[0]\AgdaComment{--\ \$\ MatchingWeightPattern.}\<%
\\
\>[0]\AgdaKeyword{data}\AgdaSpace{}%
\AgdaDatatype{MatchingWeightPattern}\AgdaSpace{}%

\section{Aut\texorpdfstring{$(K_4)$}{(K4)}-invariance on the matching
weights as a real classification theorem}

The previous block stated the consequence of automorphism invariance
on a finite list of named patterns by a table.  The block below proves
the underlying classification on the open function space of all
matching weights.  A weight is any function from the three matching
indices into $\mathbb{N}$.  The automorphism group $\mathrm{Aut}(K_4)
= S_4$ acts on the four vertices of $K_4$ and induces an action on the
three perfect matchings via the surjection $S_4 \twoheadrightarrow
S_3$; under this induced action the three matchings form a single
orbit.  We use the two adjacent transpositions
$(\text{matching-one}\ \text{matching-two})$ and
$(\text{matching-two}\ \text{matching-three})$ as generators; both
descend from $\mathrm{Aut}(K_4)$ because each corresponds to a vertex
permutation that fixes one matching and swaps the other two.  Their
fixed points are the constant weights.

\section{Vertex permutations from
\texorpdfstring{$\mathrm{Aut}(K_4)$}{Aut(K4)} as the source of the two
matching generators}

The previous block proved the classification on
\texttt{MatchingPermutation}, an abstract two-element generator
family.  The block below grounds that family in
$\mathrm{Aut}(K_4)$.  We introduce the four vertices of $K_4$, the
six edges, the membership of each edge in a perfect matching, two
specific vertex transpositions
$(\mathrm{v_1}\ \mathrm{v_2})$ and $(\mathrm{v_2}\ \mathrm{v_3})$
together with proofs that they are involutions and hence bijections,
and the generic induced action of an arbitrary vertex permutation on
matchings via its edge image.  We then check by case analysis that
the induced action of $(\mathrm{v_1}\ \mathrm{v_2})$ coincides with
the abstract generator \texttt{swap-two-three} and that the induced
action of $(\mathrm{v_2}\ \mathrm{v_3})$ coincides with
\texttt{swap-one-two}.  Composing with the previous classification
theorem yields the master statement: every weight on the three
matchings that is invariant under the two named vertex transpositions
is constant.  Since $K_4$ is the complete graph on four vertices,
every vertex bijection is an automorphism of $K_4$, so the two
specific transpositions are genuine elements of $\mathrm{Aut}(K_4)$.

uniformity theorem.  They are recorded as involutive bijections before their
effect on matchings is computed.

The last step verifies that the two named vertex bijections are genuine graph
automorphisms of $K_4$.  Since $K_4$ is complete, preserving distinctness is
enough to preserve adjacency.

The matching-level invariance used inside the previous classification
is therefore not a stipulation: it is the consequence of invariance
under two concrete vertex transpositions, each given as a
\texttt{VertexBijection}, with their induced action on matchings
computed from the vertex permutation, the edge structure and the
matching membership table.  The classification of matching-uniform
weights is now reduced to: \emph{any matching weight invariant under
$(\mathrm{v_1}\ \mathrm{v_2})$ and $(\mathrm{v_2}\ \mathrm{v_3})$ is
constant}.  Combined with the separator filter, the bare/running/tower
uniformity locks and the absence of any $K_4$-intrinsic mechanism
that breaks vertex-level invariance, the bare structural ratio
$\text{middle}/\text{heaviest} = E = 6$ is forced through every
$K_4$-intrinsic, $\mathrm{Aut}(K_4)$-invariant layer of the schema.

This is a real classification theorem on an open function space, not
a table over a finite list of named patterns.  The earlier finite
table is therefore now backed by an underlying mathematical statement:
the constraint \emph{Aut$(K_4)$-invariant on the three perfect
matchings} is preserved by the constant weights and by no other.  In
particular, no $K_4$-intrinsic, $\mathrm{Aut}(K_4)$-invariant
multiplicative reweighting of the matchings can produce a
matching-differential factor between $m_2$ and $m_3$.  The bare
structural ratio middle / heaviest $= E = 6$ is therefore forced not
just on the named candidates but on every weight function in
$\mathbb{N}^{\mathrm{Matching}}$ that respects the automorphism
action.

\section{Neutral residual carrier classification}

The matching theorem closes the tempting but wrong local move.  A
neutral residual that changes the splitting ratio cannot be carried by
matching weights, because any $K_4$-intrinsic matching weight that
respects the vertex automorphisms is uniform across the three perfect
matchings.  The residual carrier must therefore act one layer above the
matching index.

The finite candidate space has exactly three entries.  There may be no
residual carrier; the residual may be placed directly on the matchings;
or the residual may be carried by the already available coupling
complement.  The four filters are the closure contract of this section:
the carrier must be splitting-asymmetric, must preserve the
$\mathrm{Aut}(K_4)$-uniformity of matching weights, must use a ledger
complement already present, and must introduce no empirical patch.  Under
those filters only the coupling-complement carrier survives.  This is the
formal closure of the carrier slot; the physical reading as a neutrino
splitting correction remains an empirical test.

\begin{code}%
\>[0]\AgdaComment{--\ \$\ NeutralResidualCarrier.}\<%
\\

splits $306$ into the active loop part $11$ and its complement $295$.

The carrier classification packages the survivor, the matching theorem, the
complement ledger, and the effective separator ratio.  The physical reading
of that ratio remains explicitly marked as a test.

\section{Final residual-corrected neutrino prediction}

The tower-corrected Planck bridge fixes the heaviest neutral ratio.  The
neutral residual carrier does not change that scale; it changes the
separator between the two nonzero neutral masses.  The old edge separator
$E=6$ is replaced at the final neutral readout by
\[
  E_\nu = 6\cdot\frac{295}{306} = \frac{1770}{306}.
\]
Thus
\[
  \frac{m_3}{m_2}=\frac{1770}{306},\qquad
  m_2 = m_3\cdot\frac{306}{1770}.
\]
Using the same Planck input as the tower-corrected table, the final
normal-hierarchy prediction is
\[
  m_1 = 0,
  \qquad m_2 = 0.008589080150\,\mathrm{eV},
  \qquad m_3 = 0.049681934200\,\mathrm{eV},
\]
with
\[
  \sum_i m_i = 0.058271014350\,\mathrm{eV},
\]
and
\[
  \Delta m^2_{21} = 7.3772297820\cdot10^{-5}\,\mathrm{eV}^2,
  \qquad
  \Delta m^2_{31} = 2.4682945859\cdot10^{-3}\,\mathrm{eV}^2.
\]
Equivalently, the squared-splitting ratio is approximately
$33.45828527$.  These decimals are the physical readout of the exact
Planck ratios below; they are not used as filters.

The neutral sector occupies a separate but parallel structure.  The
two observed splittings $\Delta m^2_{21}$ and $\Delta m^2_{31}$ are
labelled by ordered pairs of generation indices on the leptonic side.
The cell table predicts a third splitting $\Delta m^2_{32}$, which is
not an independent observable; it is fixed by the other two as
$\Delta m^2_{31} - \Delta m^2_{21}$.  This redundancy is recorded
formally so that the cell table cannot smuggle in an extra free
parameter.

\section{Charge duality and the elementary slot count}

The cell table classified above carries a side index and a matching
index.  Inside each cell a third refinement is forced by the material
already fixed above.  The neutral-depth survivor lives on the neutral side of
the cell, and the charged-sector ratios live on the charged side.  Without a
charged-versus-neutral split inside each cell, the neutral-depth carrier and
the charged-mass carrier would collide in the same slot.  The split is
therefore not an addition; it is the formal consequence of keeping both
surviving carriers separate.

The candidate space below names the obvious alternatives.  A trivial
duality has only one charge state and erases the neutral-depth
carrier.  A foreign $U(1)$ adds a continuous structure not visible in
$K_4$ and is excluded by the same discipline that excluded a foreign
group in the previous section.  Only the intrinsic $\mathbf{Z}_2$
charge duality survives.

The elementary slot count is then the product of the three closed
finite refinements: side count, matching count, and charge-duality
count.  Each factor is fixed by its own classification above.  The
product is locked at twelve.

product, and the surviving charge-duality candidate.  The split into
charged and neutral content per cell is named explicitly so that any
any assignment must respect it.  The hadronic and leptonic
identifications of individual elementary slots remain physical
readings.

\section{Slot-fill completeness}

The elementary slot structure is finite by construction.  A separate
question is whether the structure is allowed to leave slots empty.  An
empty slot would mean that a side, matching and charge state combination
is admitted by the classification but assigned no physical content.  In
the present finite scheme this is forbidden: every admitted combination
arises from a forced refinement, and a forced refinement that produces
an unfilled cell would re-introduce a free choice through the back door.
The completeness predicate below records this as a single classification
with two candidates and a unique survivor.

\section{Spectral signature of \texorpdfstring{$K_4$}{K4}: a structural source for $1+3$}

The adjacency operator of $K_4$ has the form $A = J - I$, where $J$ is
the all-ones matrix on four vertices.  Its spectrum is fixed by graph
data alone: the trace vanishes (no self-loops), the trace of $A^2$
equals $2|E| = 12$ (twice the edge count), and the Perron eigenvalue
equals the regular degree $d=3$.  These three integer constraints
force the multiset $\{+3,\,-1,\,-1,\,-1\}$ as the unique spectrum.
The combinatorial Laplacian $L = dI - A$ then has the spectrum
$\{0,\,4,\,4,\,4\}$: one zero mode (the constant function on the four
vertices) and three degenerate positive modes at the value $V=4$.

Both spectra split the four modes as $1+3$ with opposite signs.  In
the adjacency picture: one positive Perron mode against three
degenerate negative modes.  In the Laplacian picture: one zero
(non-propagating) mode against three degenerate positive modes.  This
$1+3$ split is a theorem of $K_4$, not a choice.

The split is the structural source of every $1+3$ asymmetry that
appears in the physical reading: the time-versus-space split of a
$(-,+,+,+)$ Lorentz signature, the distinguished-versus-degenerate
character of a Perron mode, and the asymmetric branch of the charged
residual classification.  The identification of the distinguished
spectral mode with the physical name ``time'' or ``hadronic sector'',
and of the three degenerate modes with ``space'' or ``leptonic
generations'', is a physical reading and is recorded as
\texttt{physical-test}.  What is closed at the formal level is only
the existence and the cardinality of the split.

\section{Spectral anchoring of the matching index and the charge duality}

The cell structure forces three perfect matchings on $K_4$.  The
adjacency spectrum of $K_4$ also forces three degenerate negative
modes.  Both counts are theorems, and both equal three.  The record
\texttt{MatchingDegenerateAnchor} below locks the equality of the two
counts as a structural identity, not a coincidence.  Which matching
corresponds to which degenerate eigenvector is a permutation freedom
inside the degenerate eigenspace, and is recorded as
\texttt{physical-test}.

The charge duality acts as a $\mathbf{Z}_2$ on the elementary slot
table.  The Laplacian spectrum of $K_4$ has exactly two distinct
eigenvalues, $0$ and $V=4$, and the gap between them is itself $V$.
The record \texttt{ChargeDualitySpectralAnchor} locks this gap as the
structural source of the binary charge axis.  The identification of
which spectral side is named ``charged'' and which is named ``neutral''
is a physical reading and is recorded as \texttt{physical-test}.

The integer twelve, already locked as the elementary slot count by the
side, matching, and charge factors, now reappears as a product of
purely spectral data: $(1+1)$ for the distinguished/degenerate split of
the adjacency spectrum, three for the degenerate eigenspace dimension,
and two for the distinct-eigenvalue count of the Laplacian.  The two
factorisations of twelve coincide as a structural identity, locked by
\texttt{coincides-with-elementary-slot-count}.

\section{Closure of the formal layer}

The capstone record below packages the closures of this paper into a
single object.  It collects the depth survivor, the Planck bridge, the
charged-sector and neutrino-sector first-order schemas, the two
residual classifications, the neutral residual carrier, the final
residual-corrected normal-hierarchy prediction, the carrier requirement,
the intrinsic ledger extension, the cell table, the charge duality, the
elementary slot count, and the slot-fill completeness.  Every component
is locked by an equation; nothing is left as an unproved field.

After this point the formal layer of the paper is closed.
The remaining content is a physical reading: the side and matching
assignment, the slot content identification, and the comparison of the
classified ratios with measured low-energy masses.  These items are
recorded with the \texttt{physical-test} status throughout the paper;
they are not formal theorems and do not weaken the closure of the
formal layer.

\section*{Formal closure and empirical boundary}

The formal layer of this paper is closed at the level displayed
here.  Inside the finite schema the neutral depth is uniquely $5$ and
its square is $25$.  The Planck route, the suppression exponents, the
running correction, the tower action, and the charged-sector corrections
are selected by finite filters.  The two residual layers are classified
as sector-asymmetric and splitting-asymmetric.  The neutral residual
carrier is further classified: matching weights are blocked by
$\mathrm{Aut}(K_4)$-uniformity, and the surviving above-matching carrier
is the coupling complement $295/306$, acting on the edge separator as
$6\cdot 295/306$.  This supplies the final residual-corrected neutrino
prediction with $m_3/m_2=1770/306$.  The intrinsic extension of $K_4$ by
its bipartition and perfect matching still supplies the charged
sign/generation cell structure, producing six cells that coincide with
the six edges, three generation slots per side, an intrinsic
$\mathbf{Z}_2$ charge duality and twelve elementary slots.  The slot-fill
completeness predicate locks that no admitted slot is structurally empty.
The capstone record \texttt{FormalSchemaClosure} packages all of these
closures into a single object; every component is locked by an equation
and nothing in the formal layer is selected by decimal agreement with
experiment.

The physical reading begins after that point.  Three items are listed
throughout the paper as \texttt{physical-test}: the side and matching
side assignment, the slot content identification, and the comparison
of the classified ratios with measured low-energy masses, oscillation
data, cosmological bounds, beta-decay limits and neutrinoless
double-beta constraints.  A failed comparison would refute the
physical reading.  It would not refute the formal layer that closes
this paper.

\section*{Empirical boundary and falsification}

The formal result closes with exact rational data.  Under the Planck-mass
and electron-mass conventions displayed in this paper, the proposed physical
readout is
\[
  m_1=0,
  \qquad m_2\approx0.008589080150\,\mathrm{eV},
  \qquad m_3\approx0.049681934200\,\mathrm{eV},
\]
\[
  \sum_i m_i\approx0.058271014350\,\mathrm{eV},
  \qquad
  \Delta m^2_{21}\approx7.377228\cdot10^{-5}\,\mathrm{eV}^2,
  \qquad
  \Delta m^2_{31}\approx2.4682945859\cdot10^{-3}\,\mathrm{eV}^2.
\]
These numbers are not fitted inputs.  They are the electronvolt rendering of
the exact Planck-ratio chain checked above.  The comparison with oscillation
fits, cosmological bounds, beta-decay searches and neutrinoless-double-beta
searches is therefore the empirical boundary of the paper, not another Agda
claim.

The comparison uses named external conventions: the Planck-mass convention,
the electron-mass value, the oscillation-data fit and confidence interval,
the cosmological mass-sum bound, and the nuclear-matrix-element convention
used when a neutrinoless-double-beta effective mass is quoted.  Changing one
of these external data states changes the comparison line, not the formal
chain.  The exact Agda ratios carry no statistical error bars; the empirical
comparison does.

The physical identification is falsified if the neutrino hierarchy is
inverted, if the lightest neutrino is forced away from zero beyond the stated
boundary, if the mass sum is excluded under the declared cosmological
convention, if the two squared splittings cannot be kept inside the declared
oscillation intervals, or if beta-decay or neutrinoless-double-beta data
exclude the effective masses implied by the normal-hierarchy, $m_1=0$
reading.  Such an outcome would reject the proposed physical interpretation
of the finite ledger.  It would not alter the checked arithmetic itself.

That is the intended exposure of the result: a closed finite schema, an exact
rational mass readout, and a physical identification that remains answerable
to measurement.
